================================================================================
  DOCUMENT CONTROL
================================================================================
  Title ............. Backstage — Complete Learning Guide for Beginners
  Edition ........... Definitive Merged Edition ("CBA Exam AND Beyond")
  Document type ..... Self-contained study + production reference (plain text)
  Aligned to ........ Official Certified Backstage Associate (CBA) curriculum,
                      Linux Foundation (4 domains, 19 official competencies)
  Reference project . easypj (Django PROJECT) . jobpost (Django APP)
  Stack ............. Python/Django (demo app) . TypeScript/React/Node.js
                      (Backstage itself) . PostgreSQL . Kubernetes
  Structure ......... 10 Parts . 32 Chapters . 9 Appendices (A-I)

================================================================================
  ABOUT THIS DOCUMENT
================================================================================
  This is the GOLD-STANDARD Backstage learning guide - a single source of truth
  for content, structure, visual language and quality bar. It takes a complete
  beginner from zero -> CBA-certified -> confident Backstage practitioner,
  WITHOUT ever needing official docs, blogs, books or videos again.

  MISSION - every concept is taught once, in depth, and demonstrated on ONE
  real application (easypj/jobpost) so knowledge compounds instead of resetting
  each chapter. Each topic carries a "CBA lens" (what is tested) AND a "Day-2
  lens" (real operations), with internals included so you never have to leave.

  INTENDED AUDIENCE
  -----------------
    - Primary ... newcomers preparing for the CBA exam (no prior Backstage
                  experience assumed).
    - Secondary . platform engineers wanting one deep, exam-AND-production
                  reference for running a developer portal day to day.

  SCOPE
  -----
    IN  ... 100% of the official CBA exam domains (Customizing 32%, Dev
            Workflow 24%, Infrastructure 22%, Catalog 22%), PLUS production
            "Day-2" operation (deployment, scaling, security, observability).
    OUT ... deep Kubernetes administration (CKA) and application-developer
            patterns unrelated to Backstage are referenced where useful but
            are not the focus.

  THE 5 PROMISES OF THIS GUIDE
  ----------------------------
    ┌───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │  1. ZERO-ASSUMPTION       Every term is defined the first time it appears.    │
    │                            No "you should already know X."                    │
    │  2. SHOW, DONT TELL        Never 3 paragraphs in a row without a diagram,     │
    │                            table, card, or code block.                        │
    │  3. ONE REAL PROJECT       Every concept is demonstrated on easypj/jobpost -  │
    │                            the SAME app, start to finish.                     │
    │  4. EXAM + BEYOND          Each topic has a "CBA lens" (what is tested) AND   │
    │                            a "Day-2 lens" (real operations use).              │
    │  5. SELF-CONTAINED         Internals included ("Under the Hood") so you       │
    │                            never need to leave this document.                 │
    └───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    WHAT IS BACKSTAGE?
    ──────────────────
    ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │                                                              │
    │   Backstage is an OPEN-SOURCE PLATFORM for building          │
    │   DEVELOPER PORTALS. Created by Spotify, donated to CNCF.    │
    │                                                              │
    │   Think of it as: "The Operating System for your            │
    │   Engineering Organization"                                  │
    │                                                              │
    │   🎯  The Problem It Solves:                                 │
    │   ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐    │
    │   │ ❌  BEFORE BACKSTAGE                               │    │
    │   │  • "Where's the docs for service X?"               │    │
    │   │  • "What APIs does team Y own?"                    │    │
    │   │  • "How do I create a new microservice?"           │    │
    │   │  • "Who owns this broken service?"                 │    │
    │   │  • 15 Slack channels, 10 wikis, 5 Jira boards      │    │
    │   │  • New dev takes 2 weeks to ship first commit      │    │
    │   └────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘    │
    │                                                              │
    │   ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐    │
    │   │ ✅  WITH BACKSTAGE                                 │    │
    │   │  • ONE place to find everything                     │    │
    │   │  • Software Catalog — all services, APIs, docs      │    │
    │   │  • Software Templates — scaffold in minutes        │    │
    │   │  • TechDocs — docs alongside code                  │    │
    │   │  • Plugins — extend for YOUR needs                  │    │
    │   │  • New dev ships first commit in 2 HOURS           │    │
    │   └────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘    │
    │                                                              │
    └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    CBA EXAM DOMAINS & COMPETENCIES (Official)
    ───────────────────────────────────────────
    ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │                                                              │
    │  📦 DOMAIN 1: CUSTOMIZING BACKSTAGE (32%)                    │
    │  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐│
    │  │  • Understand frontend versus backend plugins            ││
    │  │  • Customizing Backstage plugins                         ││
    │  │  • Make changes to React code in Backstage App           ││
    │  │  • Using Material UI components                          ││
    │  └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘│
    │                                                              │
    │  🛠️ DOMAIN 2: BACKSTAGE DEVELOPMENT WORKFLOW (24%)           │
    │  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐│
    │  │  • Build and run Backstage projects locally              ││
    │  │  • Understand local development workflows                ││
    │  │  • Compile a Backstage project with TypeScript           ││
    │  │  • Download and install dependencies with NPM/Yarn       ││
    │  │  • Use Docker to build a container image of Backstage    ││
    │  └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘│
    │                                                              │
    │  🏗️ DOMAIN 3: BACKSTAGE INFRASTRUCTURE (22%)                 │
    │  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐│
    │  │  • Understand the Backstage framework                    ││
    │  │  • Configure Backstage                                   ││
    │  │  • Deploy Backstage to production                        ││
    │  │  • Understand Backstage client-server architecture       ││
    │  └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘│
    │                                                              │
    │  📋 DOMAIN 4: BACKSTAGE CATALOG (22%)                        │
    │  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐│
    │  │  • Understand how/why to use Backstage Catalog            ││
    │  │  • Populate Backstage Catalog                             ││
    │  │  • Using annotations                                      ││
    │  │  • Working with manually registered entity locations     ││
    │  │  • Troubleshooting entity ingestion                      ││
    │  │  • Working with automated ingestion                      ││
    │  └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘│
    │                                                              │
    └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    BACKSTAGE VS OTHER DEVELOPER PORTALS
    ─────────────────────────────────────
    ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │  Feature            Backstage    Confluence   Custom Portal   │
    │  ─────────────────  ──────────   ──────────   ─────────────  │
    │  Open Source        ✅  Yes      ❌  No       🟡  Maybe       │
    │  CNCF Project        ✅  Yes      ❌  No       ❌  No          │
    │  Plugin Ecosystem   ✅  200+     ❌  Limited   🔴  None       │
    │  Software Catalog   ✅  Built-in ❌  Manual    🔴  Build      │
    │  Software Templates ✅  Built-in ❌  None      🔴  Build      │
    │  TechDocs (MDX)     ✅  Built-in ❌  Confluence 🔴  None      │
    │  Kubernetes Native  ✅  Plugin   ❌  None      🔴  Build      │
    │  GitOps Friendly    ✅  Native   ❌  None      🟡  Possible   │
    │  Customizable UI    ✅  React    ❌  Limited   🟡  Depends    │
    │  API-First Design   ✅  REST     ❌  None      🟡  Depends    │
    └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    🏗️ BACKSTAGE — THE AIRPORT ANALOGY
    ────────────────────────────────────
    ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │                                                              │
    │  Think of Backstage as a MODERN AIRPORT HUB:                 │
    │                                                              │
    │  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐   │
    │  │              🛫 THE AIRPORT (Backstage)               │   │
    │  │                                                      │   │
    │  │  ┌─────────────┐  ┌─────────────┐  ┌─────────────┐  │   │
    │  │  │ 📋 Flight   │  │ 🧳 Baggage  │  │ 🛍️ Duty-Free│  │   │
    │  │  │ Information │  │   Claim     │  │   Shops     │  │   │
    │  │  │ (Catalog)   │  │ (Templates) │  │  (Plugins)  │  │   │
    │  │  └─────────────┘  └─────────────┘  └─────────────┘  │   │
    │  │                                                      │   │
    │  │  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────┐   │   │
    │  │  │        🛂 SECURITY CHECKPOINT (Auth/RBAC)     │   │   │
    │  │  └──────────────────────────────────────────────┘   │   │
    │  │                                                      │   │
    │  │  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────┐   │   │
    │  │  │  🛄 BAGGAGE HANDLING (CI/CD Pipeline)         │   │   │
    │  │  └──────────────────────────────────────────────┘   │   │
    │  └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘   │
    │                                                              │
    │  🛫 AIRPORT SERVICES                  BACKSTAGE FEATURES     │
    │  ─────────────────────────────────    ────────────────────   │
    │  Flight Info Board (Arrivals/Dep)  →  Software Catalog      │
    │  Luggage Handling System           →  Software Templates    │
    │  Duty-Free Shops & Restaurants     →  Plugin Ecosystem      │
    │  Security Checkpoint               →  Authentication/RBAC   │
    │  Baggage Conveyor Belt             →  CI/CD Pipeline        │
    │  Information Kiosks                →  TechDocs/Search       │
    │  Air Traffic Control Tower         →  Backstage Backend     │
    │  Terminal Gates                    →  Entity Pages          │
    │                                                              │
    │  💡 Just like an airport connects passengers to flights,    │
    │     Backstage connects developers to their tools & services │
    └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    🏗️ BACKSTAGE — THE SHOPPING MALL ANALOGY
    ──────────────────────────────────────────
    ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │                                                              │
    │  Think of Backstage as a MASSIVE SHOPPING MALL:              │
    │                                                              │
    │  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐   │
    │  │           🏬 THE BACKSTAGE MALL                       │   │
    │  │                                                      │   │
    │  │  ┌────────┐ ┌────────┐ ┌────────┐ ┌────────┐       │   │
    │  │  │ Nike   │ │ Apple  │ │  Zara  │ │  H&M   │       │   │
    │  │  │ (GitHub)│ │(Jenkins)│ │(Sentry)│ │(Jira)  │       │   │
    │  │  └────────┘ └────────┘ └────────┘ └────────┘       │   │
    │  │                                                      │   │
    │  │  ┌────────────────────────────────────────────┐     │   │
    │  │  │     🗺️ MALL DIRECTORY (Software Catalog)    │     │   │
    │  │  │    "Find any store/service in seconds"      │     │   │
    │  │  └────────────────────────────────────────────┘     │   │
    │  │                                                      │   │
    │  │  ┌────────────────────────────────────────────┐     │   │
    │  │  │  🏗️ NEW STORE BUILDER (Software Templates)  │     │   │
    │  │  │   "Set up a new store in minutes"           │     │   │
    │  │  └────────────────────────────────────────────┘     │   │
    │  └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘   │
    │                                                              │
    │  Each "store" = A tool integrated into Backstage            │
    │  Mall directory = Software Catalog                          │
    │  New store builder = Software Templates                     │
    │  Mall map kiosk = Search                                    │
    │  Security guards = RBAC/Auth                                │
    │                                                              │
    │  💡 Just like a mall brings ALL stores under ONE roof,     │
    │     Backstage brings ALL developer tools under ONE UI       │
    └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    🔄 THE DEVELOPER EXPERIENCE — Before vs After Backstage
    ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │                                                              │
    │  ❌  THE FRAGMENTED WORLD (Before Backstage)                 │
    │  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐   │
    │  │                                                      │   │
    │  │  👨‍💻 Developer                                         │   │
    │  │     │                                                │   │
    │  │     ├──→ GitHub (find repo)... 10 min                │   │
    │  │     ├──→ Jenkins (check CI)... 15 min                │   │
    │  │     ├──→ Confluence (find docs)... 20 min            │   │
    │  │     ├──→ Jira (find tickets)... 10 min               │   │
    │  │     ├──→ PagerDuty (on-call)... 5 min                │   │
    │  │     ├──→ Slack (ask questions)... 15 min             │   │
    │  │     ├──→ Grafana (check metrics)... 10 min           │   │
    │  │     ├──→ Sentry (check errors)... 10 min             │   │
    │  │     └──→ kubectl (check K8s)... 10 min               │   │
    │  │                                                      │   │
    │  │  TOTAL CONTEXT-SWITCHING: 10+ tools, ~2 hours/day    │   │
    │  └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘   │
    │                                                              │
    │                    ⬇️  BACKSTAGE TRANSFORMS THIS  ⬇️          │

    │  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐   │

    │  │                │ 🚀 Templates     │ ← Scaffolding    │   │
    │  │                │ 📖 TechDocs      │ ← Documentation  │   │

    LEARNING PATH OVERVIEW
    ──────────────────────
    [ROADMAP ILLUSTRATION: 10-Part journey from zero to Backstage expert]

    Prerequisites:
      • Basic understanding of JavaScript/TypeScript (React)
      • Familiarity with terminal / command line
      • Basic YAML knowledge
      • Node.js 18+ installed
      • Docker installed (for local development)
      • A computer with 8 GB+ RAM
      [ICON ROW: Node.js logo | TypeScript logo | YAML icon | Docker icon | Terminal icon]

    How to Use This Guide:
      • Each chapter builds on the previous one
      • Every concept includes easypj/jobpost real examples
      • 🟢 = Beginner | 🟡 = Intermediate | 🔴 = Advanced
      • 🎯 = CBA Exam Topic (directly tested)
      • [INTERACTIVE PROGRESS TRACKER BAR]

  SYMBOL & ICON LEGEND
================================================================================
    Every icon below carries a FIXED meaning across the whole guide so your
    eye learns to scan without re-reading labels every time.

    NAVIGATION & STATUS
    ┌───────────┬────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │  🎯       │ CBA exam topic (graded competency)                         │
    │  🏗️      │ Beyond-the-exam / production Day-2 knowledge               │
    │  🧪       │ Hands-on / practice task                                   │
    │  ⏱️       │ Time budget / pacing note                                  │
    │  🟢 🟡 🔴 │ Beginner / Intermediate / Advanced difficulty              │
    └───────────┴────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    TEACHING CUES
    ┌───────────┬────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │  💡       │ Key insight / "aha" moment                                 │
    │  🧠       │ Mental model / analogy                                     │
    │  🔖       │ Definition of a term (Key Term box)                        │
    │  🔧       │ Under the Hood (internals / how it really works)           │
    │  ⚠️       │ Gotcha / common mistake / trap                             │
    │  ⚡        │ Speed tip (shortcut, time-saver)                           │
    │  🔑       │ Must-remember command / config field / fact                │
    │  ✅ ❌      │ Do / Don't . Correct / Wrong                               │
    │  🧭       │ Decision guide ("which one do I use?")                     │
    └───────────┴────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    BACKSTAGE OBJECT ICONS  (reused in every architecture diagram)
    ┌───────────┬────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │  📦       │ Software Catalog - the registry of all tracked entities    │
    │  🧩       │ Component - a service, website, library, or tool           │
    │  📡       │ API - a REST/GraphQL/gRPC interface an entity exposes      │
    │  💾       │ Resource - infra a Component depends on (DB, cache...)     │
    │  👤       │ User - a person entity (auto-discovered from auth)         │
    │  👥       │ Group - a team, squad, or org unit                         │
    │  🖥️      │ System - groups related Components/APIs/Resources          │
    │  🏢       │ Domain - groups related Systems (a business area)          │
    │  🚀       │ Template - scaffolds new projects via the Scaffolder       │
    │  🔌       │ Plugin - a frontend and/or backend extension module        │
    │  📖       │ TechDocs - Markdown docs published beside the code         │
    │  🔐       │ Auth - sign-in provider (GitHub/Okta/Google/...)           │
    │  ☸️       │ Kubernetes (Ch10+) - cluster the K8s plugin reads from;    │
    │           │ also used for Backstage-the-platform in front-matter art   │
    │  🐘       │ PostgreSQL - easypj/jobpost's production database          │
    │  🐍       │ easypj - the Django PROJECT used as this guide's demo      │
    │  💼       │ jobpost - the Django APP inside easypj (the job board)     │
    └───────────┴────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘


================================================================================
  THE VISUAL COMPONENT CATALOG  (the art direction)
================================================================================
    These are the building blocks used to render content throughout this
    guide. The rule: "NEVER a wall of text." Every subsection uses at least
    one of these. Each entry = [WHAT it is] + [WHEN to use it].

    1.  CONCEPT CARD GRID          2-4 cards, icon + title + 1-line idea.
        WHEN: introducing a set of sibling concepts (e.g. the 4 catalog
        entity kinds: Component, API, Resource, Group).

    2.  ANATOMY CALLOUT             label every field of a YAML/command.
        WHEN: the first time a catalog-info.yaml, template.yaml, or
        app-config.yaml block is shown.

    3.  COMPARISON TABLE           side-by-side feature matrix.
        WHEN: "X vs Y" decisions (Backstage vs Confluence, CBA vs CKA).

    4.  DECISION TREE 🧭           branching "which one do I use?" guide.
        WHEN: choosing among options (build a plugin vs use an existing one).

    5.  SEQUENCE / FLOW DIAGRAM    numbered steps with arrows (left->right
        or top->down). WHEN: a process over time (a click's request/response
        round trip, the catalog ingestion pipeline).

    6.  ARCHITECTURE DIAGRAM       boxed components + connections + legend.
        WHEN: showing how parts of the system relate spatially (frontend/
        backend/database layers, the Catalog/System/Component hierarchy).

    7.  MENTAL-MODEL / ANALOGY 🧠  relate Backstage to everyday life.
        WHEN: a concept is abstract (the airport hub, the shopping mall).

    8.  COMMAND / CONFIG CHEAT-CARD 🔑  grouped, copy-paste commands and
        config keys with notes. WHEN: end of every chapter.

    9.  YAML SKELETON (annotated)  minimal manifest, every field commented.
        WHEN: teaching the shape of a catalog-info.yaml, template.yaml, or
        app-config.yaml entry; also used inside lab answers.

    10. UNDER THE HOOD 🔧          deep-dive (ingestion internals, plugin
        lifecycle, request/response paths). WHEN: once per chapter.

    11. GOTCHA BOX ⚠️              the trap + why it happens + the fix.
        WHEN: a place learners reliably fail (wrong branch, missing token,
        stale annotation).

    12. SPEED TIP ⚡               a shortcut that saves setup or exam time.
        WHEN: an imperative/CLI trick beats hand-writing config.

    13. DAY-2 OPS BOX 🏢           "in a real job you would..." scenario.
        WHEN: connecting a tested skill to actual platform operation.

    14. TROUBLESHOOTING FLOW       symptom -> checks -> cause -> fix tree.
        WHEN: any failure mode (entity never appears, TechDocs 404, template
        scaffold fails).

    15. BEFORE / AFTER SPLIT       "without Backstage" vs "with Backstage"
        two-panel. WHEN: motivating why a feature exists.

    16. KEY TAKEAWAYS BANNER       ✅ bullet recap + 🎯 CBA relevance +
        🔑 commands/config. WHEN: end of every chapter AND every part.

    17. VISUAL SUMMARY             one big picture that re-tells the whole
        chapter/part in a single diagram, always paired with a short prose
        recap. WHEN: closing every chapter/part.

    18. PRACTICE DRILL 🧪          short timed checklist task (do-it-now).
        WHEN: mid/after a chapter, before the formal exam-style questions.

    19. CBA EXAM PRACTICE QUESTIONS CARD 🧪  question + Answer + Explanation
        (with YAML/diagram where useful). WHEN: 10+ per chapter, at the end
        (this is the existing "CBA Exam Practice Questions" block already
        used throughout the guide).

    20. KEY TERM / GLOSSARY POP 📖  bold term + tight definition box.
        WHEN: the first time a Backstage-specific term appears.

    Every chapter is assembled from these blocks IN THIS ORDER. Existing
    chapters already contain [F] (the numbered Concept Sections) and [M]
    (CBA Exam Practice Questions) — the other blocks are the new wrapper
    this guide is adopting around that existing content.

      [A] CHAPTER HEADER      Title + difficulty meta (🟢/🟡/🔴, ⏱️ est time,
                              🎯 CBA domain %, tags) — the META line.

                              Before/After or analogy visual.

      [D] CBA RELEVANCE       Which exact official CBA competencies (Domain
                              1-4) this chapter maps to.
      [E] SECTION-MAP DIAGRAM A single diagram showing how all sections of
                              THIS chapter relate (the "you are here" map).
      [F] CONCEPT SECTIONS    The numbered sections (x.1, x.2, ...). Each one:
                                . 📖 key terms . 🧠 analogy where useful
                                . core explanation broken up by visuals
                                . annotated YAML/config/commands as relevant
                                . ⚠️ gotcha . ⚡ speed tip . 🏢 Day-2 note
      [G] UNDER THE HOOD 🔧   One internals deep-dive for the chapter.
      [H] KEY TAKEAWAYS 🔑    ✅ recap + 🎯 CBA relevance.
      [I] EXAM GOTCHAS ⚠️     The top traps for this chapter, listed.
      [J] SPEED TIPS ⚡       Shortcuts / CLI patterns that save time.
      [K] COMMANDS/CONFIG TO REMEMBER 🔑  Cheat-card of must-know commands
                              and config fields.
      [L] PRACTICE DRILL 🧪   A short, timed do-it-now checklist.
      [M] CBA EXAM PRACTICE QUESTIONS 🧪  >=10 exam-style Q&A (the existing
                              block already present at the end of every
                              chapter). EACH has: Question, Answer, and an
                              Explanation of the WHY.
      [N] VISUAL SUMMARY      One closing big-picture diagram + prose recap.

                              with a short prose recap.


================================================================================
  THE RUNNING PROJECT: easypj / jobpost
================================================================================
    Everything in this guide is taught on ONE real application so concepts
    compound instead of resetting each chapter (Ch 1.12).

    WHAT IT IS
      📖 easypj   = the Django PROJECT (the repo root, the deployable unit).
      📖 jobpost  = the Django APP inside it — a job-board application where
                    employers post jobs and users filter by Skills, Location,
                    and Author (Ch 1.12).

    THE UNDERLYING DATA MODEL

    ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │ EASYPJ / JOBPOST -- THE UNDERLYING DJANGO DATA MODEL            │
    │                                                                 │
    │   User (django.contrib.auth)                                    │
    │      |                                                          │
    │      | 1-to-1 profile link                                      │
    │      v                                                          │
    │   Author  -- an employer / job-poster account                   │
    │      |                                                          │
    │      | 1-to-many postings                                       │
    │      v                                                          │
    │   JobPost  -- the core listing (jobpost/models.py)              │
    │      ^                                  ^                       │
    │      | many-to-one                     | many-to-many           │
    │   Location                           Skills                     │
    │   (where the job is)            (what the job requires)         │
    │                                                                 │
    │   jobpost REST surface (Ch 9, 13): /api/jobs/ and /api/skills/  │
    │   jobpost-api (Backstage API entity) description: "REST API for │
    │   job postings -- filter by skills/location" (Ch 8.2)           │
    └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    PROSE SUMMARY — jobpost is a small Django app with four real models:
    a built-in Django User, an Author profile linked 1-to-1 to that User,
    the core JobPost listing linked many-to-1 to a Location and many-to-many
    to a set of Skills. jobpost's own REST endpoints (/api/jobs/,
    /api/skills/) are what later become the jobpost-api API entity in the
    Backstage Catalog (Ch 8.2, 9) — the exact same filtering logic
    ("by skills/location") is documented on both the Django API and its
    Backstage API entity description.

    WHERE EACH PIECE APPEARS IN BACKSTAGE  (Ch 1.12 mapping, in full)
      Django app              -> Component entity        (Ch 3, 8)
      REST endpoints          -> API entity               (Ch 8, 9)
      PostgreSQL database     -> Resource entity          (Ch 3, 8)
      /docs folder            -> Published TechDocs       (Ch 14-15)
      k8s/ manifests          -> Live pod health tab       (Ch 10, 20)
      New feature branch      -> Scaffolded via Template   (Ch 11-13)

    TARGET ARCHITECTURE  (what the guide builds by the end, Ch 6, 8, App G)

    ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │ TARGET ARCHITECTURE -- easypj REGISTERED IN THE BACKSTAGE CATALOG              │
    │                                                                                │
    │   hr-domain (Domain, Ch 8.3)                                                   │
    │     +-- easypj-system (System, Ch 8.3)                                         │
    │           +-- jobpost (Component: service, owner: team-platform, Ch 8.1)       │
    │           |     +-- providesApis -> jobpost-api (API: openapi, Ch 8.2)         │
    │           |     +-- dependsOn    -> postgres-db (Resource: database, Ch 8.5)   │
    │           |     +-- techdocs-ref: dir:.       -> TechDocs tab   (Ch 14-15)     │
    │           |     +-- kubernetes-id: jobpost    -> K8s tab        (Ch 10, 19-20) │
    │           +-- team-platform (Group, owner of all of the above, Ch 8.4)         │
    │                                                                                │
    │   Registered separately, all pointed at the SAME jobpost repo:                 │
    │     Location (Ch 7.3/7.5)  -> the catalog-info.yaml file above                 │
    │     Templates (Ch 11-13, App G.5): django-service, react-frontend,             │
    │                                     db-migration-job, infra-as-code            │
    │     TechDocs (Ch 14-15, App G.4): mkdocs.yml + docs/ -> published docs         │
    │     Kubernetes plugin (Ch 10, 19-20): kubernetes-id -> live pod health         │
    └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    PROSE SUMMARY — by the end of this guide, jobpost is not just running
    code; it is fully "onboarded" into Backstage. One Component entity
    (jobpost) sits inside easypj-system, owned by team-platform, and carries
    four live connections: it PROVIDES the jobpost-api API entity, it
    DEPENDS ON the postgres-db Resource entity, its techdocs-ref annotation
    publishes its docs into the TechDocs tab, and its kubernetes-id
    annotation lights up the Kubernetes tab with live pod health. Separately,
    four Scaffolder Templates let new services be created the same way
    jobpost was (Ch 11-13, App G.5) — closing the loop from "one demo app"
    to "a repeatable platform pattern."

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
PART 1: FOUNDATION — UNDERSTANDING BACKSTAGE                            🟢
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

  [PART 1 INTRO]
  Before you write a single line of Backstage code you need to see WHY it
  exists and WHAT it actually is. Part 1 builds that picture from nothing:
  the developer-experience problem it solves, the three-layer architecture
  that runs it, the vocabulary (Entity, Kind, Annotation...) every later
  chapter assumes you already know, and finally your own hands running a
  real instance on your laptop. We anchor every idea in easypj/jobpost so
  none of it stays abstract.

  This is the true "foundation" -- Parts 2 onward (the Catalog, Templates,
  TechDocs, Plugins, production hardening) all assume you already think in
  the language built here. Skipping ahead because Part 1 "looks easy" is
  the single most common reason CBA candidates stumble: they can execute a
  step but cannot explain WHY it works, and the exam tests the why.

  ANALOGY. Think of Backstage the way you'd think of a city opening its
  first public library. Before Part 1, the city's books (services) are
  scattered across a hundred desks, hallways and back rooms -- Git repos,
  wikis, Slack threads -- with no card catalog anywhere. Part 1 is the
  moment the city opens ONE library: it explains what the card catalog is
  (Ch1-3), how the building itself is wired -- front desk, stacks, basement
  archive (Ch2's frontend/backend/database) -- and finally hands you the
  keys to open your own branch (Ch4-6).

  [HOW THE CHAPTERS FIT -- Part 1 map]
    ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │ Ch1 WHAT ──────▶ Ch2 HOW ──────▶ Ch3 VOCAB                         │
    │  the big idea        the guts         the words                    │
    │      │                                     │                       │
    │      └─────────────────┬───────────────────┘                       │
    │                         ▼                                          │
    │         Ch4 SETUP ──▶ Ch5 CONFIGURE ──▶ Ch6 BUILD                  │
    │         install it     master the CLI    ship a real easypj portal │
    └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

  Read the map as a sentence: we ask WHAT Backstage is, learn HOW it is
  built, and learn to SPEAK its vocabulary (Ch1-3) -- then we INSTALL it,
  CONFIGURE it, and BUILD a real instance for easypj (Ch4-6). Each chapter
  is a prerequisite for the next -- do them in order.

  DECISION GUIDE -- where do I start?
    ┌───────────────────────────────────────────┬───────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │ If you...                                 │ Then...                                           │
    ├───────────────────────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
    │ have never used a developer portal before │ start at Ch1 and do not skip Ch3 (terminology)    │
    │ know Docker/K8s but not Backstage         │ skim Ch1-2, slow down at Ch3 (the vocabulary)     │
    │ already ran 'create-app' once             │ jump to Ch5 (CLI & config), then Ch6 (build it)   │
    │ are revising for the CBA exam             │ read every chapter's Key Takeaways + Drill blocks │
    └───────────────────────────────────────────┴───────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

  [PART 1 SUMMARY + IMPLEMENTATION -- read before the chapters]
  By the end of Part 1 you will have nothing conceptually mysterious left:
  you can explain what Backstage is and is not, describe its three-layer
  architecture and 5 core services, read any catalog-info.yaml and name
  every field, and -- critically -- you will have actually run
  `npx @backstage/create-app` and have a real easypj-portal serving the
  jobpost Component at http://localhost:3000. Implementation thread: easypj
  goes from "a Django app nobody outside the team can find" to "a Component,
  an API, and a Resource, visible and searchable in your own running
  Backstage instance."

Chapter 1: What is Backstage? (The Big Picture) 🎯 CBA 24% (Dev Workflow)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

  [META] Difficulty: Beginner . Estimated time: ~90 min . CBA Domain: 24% (Dev Workflow)

  [WHY THIS MATTERS]
       Right now, if a new hire joined the easypj team, they'd spend their
       first two weeks doing exactly what 1.1 describes -- hunting for the
       jobpost repo, guessing who owns it, and asking in Slack where the
       API docs live. This chapter is the case for WHY an org building
       easypj needs a developer portal at all, and gives you the
       vocabulary to talk about one before you touch a single command.

  [OBJECTIVES] After this chapter you can:
       - explain, in one sentence, the developer-experience problem
         Backstage solves (1.1)
       - give the official definition of Backstage and name its creator
         and CNCF status (1.2, 1.7)
       - list Backstage's key benefits and name its 4 Pillars (1.3, 1.8)
       - pick the right Pillar (Catalog/Templates/TechDocs/Plugins) for a
         given question (1.8)
       - use the first 12 pieces of Backstage vocabulary correctly (1.9)
       - describe what happens end-to-end when a user clicks something in
         Backstage (1.10)
       - separate "Backstage runs on Kubernetes" from "Backstage displays
         Kubernetes data" (1.11)

  [CBA RELEVANCE]
       Chapter 1 is tagged CBA 24% (Dev Workflow) -- the exam's
       foundational questions on what Backstage is, its history/CNCF
       status, and its 4 Pillars all draw directly from 1.2, 1.7 and 1.8.

  [SECTION MAP -- you are here]
       1.1 the problem -> 1.2 defined -> 1.3 benefits -> 1.4 vs other tools
       -> 1.5 workflow fit -> 1.6 real-world use -> 1.7 CNCF -> 1.8 4
       pillars -> 1.9 terminology -> 1.10 how it runs -> 1.11 Backstage+K8s
       -> 1.12 meet easypj -> 1.13 myths -> 1.14 practice Qs.

    1.1  The Problem Backstage Solves
         - Developer experience before Backstage: chaos, scattered tools
         - The "cognitive load" problem — too many tools, too many places
         - Spotify's story: 100+ squads, 1000+ services, no central portal
         - How new developers spent 2 weeks just finding things
         - The "Where's Waldo?" problem in large engineering orgs
         [ILLUSTRATION: Fragmented tooling landscape before Backstage]

         [PAIN POINT DIAGRAM — The Developer's Day BEFORE Backstage]
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  😰 A Developer's Day WITHOUT Backstage                      │
         │                                                              │
         │  "Where is the easypj repo?" → Search GitHub... 10 min       │
         │  "What's the CI/CD pipeline?" → Check Jenkins... 15 min      │
         │  "Who owns jobpost service?" → Ask in Slack... 20 min        │
         │  "Where are the API docs?" → Search Confluence... 30 min     │
         │  "How do I create a new service?" → Find template... 45 min  │
         │  "What's the PagerDuty for this?" → Ask ops team... 15 min   │
         │  ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────   │
         │  TOTAL TIME LOST: ~2.5 HOURS just finding information!       │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         [PAIN POINT DIAGRAM — The Developer's Day WITH Backstage]
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  🎉 A Developer's Day WITH Backstage                         │
         │                                                              │
         │  "Search for easypj" → Software Catalog... 30 sec            │
         │  "CI/CD status" → Built-in plugin widget... 10 sec           │
         │  "Who owns jobpost?" → Entity page → Ownership card... 5 sec │
         │  "API docs" → TechDocs tab → auto-published... 10 sec        │

    1.2  Backstage Defined — The Simple Answer
         - Official definition (verbatim, backstage.io): "Backstage is an open source framework for building developer portals" — note it is an open source FRAMEWORK, not just a "platform"
         - What is a Developer Portal? Single pane of glass for your tech ecosystem
         - Backstage = Catalog + Templates + TechDocs + Plugins
         - Created by Spotify (internal name: "Backstage" = behind the scenes)
         - Donated to CNCF in 2020 (Sandbox → Incubating since March 2022 — still Incubating, not Graduated)
         - Built with TypeScript + React + Node.js
         [CARD: Quick facts — Creator, Year, CNCF status, Language, License]

         [THE BACKSTAGE ELEVATOR PITCH]
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  "Backstage is the ANDROID/iOS OF DEVELOPER TOOLS"           │
         │                                                              │
         │  📱 Android/iOS gives you:                                   │
         │     • App Store (discover) → Software Catalog (discover)     │
         │     • SDK for building apps → Plugin framework               │
         │     • Home screen widgets → Dashboard cards                  │
         │     • System settings → Configuration                        │
         │                                                              │
         │  ☸️  Backstage gives your engineering org:                    │
         │     • Discover all services, APIs, docs in ONE place         │
         │     • Scaffold new projects with Software Templates          │
         │     • Document everything with TechDocs (docs-as-code)       │
         │     • Extend infinitely with 200+ plugins                    │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    1.3  Key Benefits of Backstage
         - Centralized Discovery — Find any service, API, team in seconds
         - Developer Self-Service — Scaffold new projects without ops tickets
         - Documentation-as-Code — Docs live next to code, auto-published
         - Single Pane of Glass — One UI for ALL your tools
         - Plugin Ecosystem — 200+ community plugins, build your own
         - Kubernetes Native — Auto-discover K8s resources
         - GitOps Friendly — Everything in Git, everything as YAML
         - Enterprise Ready — RBAC, SSO, audit logging

    1.8  The 4 Pillars — Which One Do I Reach For?
         - You already met the 4 Pillars (Catalog/Templates/TechDocs/Plugins)
         - This section is a DECISION GUIDE: given a real question, which
           pillar answers it? Novices often confuse these at first.

         [PILLAR DECISION GUIDE — Match Your Question to the Right Pillar]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  ❓ YOUR QUESTION                          ➡️  PILLAR TO USE        │
         │  ────────────────────────────────────────  ──────────────────      │
         │  "Does this service already exist?"        📦 Software Catalog     │
         │  "Who owns this API?"                      📦 Software Catalog     │
         │  "I need a brand-new microservice"         🚀 Software Templates   │
         │  "How do I set up a repo the RIGHT way?"   🚀 Software Templates   │
         │  "How does this API actually work?"        📖 TechDocs             │
         │  "Where's the deployment guide?"           📖 TechDocs             │
         │  "I want to see CI/CD status here too"     🔌 Plugins              │
         │  "I want PagerDuty info on this page"      🔌 Plugins              │
         │                                                                    │
         │  🎯 CBA TIP: exam scenario questions almost always map to exactly  │
         │     ONE pillar. Ask yourself: am I finding, creating,              │
         │     understanding, or extending? That single word is the answer.   │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    1.9  Terminology Starter Pack — Your First 12 Words
         - Backstage has its own vocabulary. Learn these 12 words now and
           every later chapter will make far more sense.
         - Full A-Z glossary lives in Appendix F — this is just the starter kit

         [YOUR FIRST 12 BACKSTAGE WORDS]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  TERM          PLAIN-ENGLISH MEANING                    MEET AGAIN IN  │
         │  ────────────  ───────────────────────────────────────  ─────────────  │
         │  Entity        Any "thing" Backstage tracks (a service,  Ch 3, 7       │
         │                a database, a team, a person...)                        │
         │  Kind          The TYPE of entity (Component, API, ...)   Ch 3, 8      │
         │  Catalog       The searchable list of ALL entities        Ch 7         │
         │  Component     An entity for software YOU build            Ch 3, 8     │
         │  Resource      An entity for infrastructure you USE         Ch 3, 8    │
         │  Plugin        An installable feature/extension             Ch 16-18   │
         │  Template      A blueprint for scaffolding new projects      Ch 11-13  │
         │  Scaffolder    The engine that RUNS templates                Ch 11     │
         │  TechDocs      Docs-as-code, published from Markdown         Ch 14-15  │
         │  Annotation    A key:value tag linking to an external tool   Ch 3, 9   │
         │  Frontend      The React app you see in your browser         Ch 2      │
         │  Backend       The Node.js server that does the real work    Ch 2      │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         [INFO BOX] 💡 One sentence that unlocks 80% of the vocabulary:
         "An Entity is a THING, a Kind is its TYPE, and an Annotation is
         a STICKY NOTE that tells a Plugin where to find more info about it."

    1.10 How Backstage Actually Runs — A Beginner's Walkthrough
         - Skip the jargon for a moment: what LITERALLY happens when you
           click something in Backstage? A simplified walkthrough.
         - Full technical version (with code) is in Chapter 2

         [A DAY IN THE LIFE OF ONE CLICK — Simplified for Beginners]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  1️⃣  You open backstage.easypj.com in your browser                    │
         │       ⬇                                                                │
         │  2️⃣  The page you see is just a React app (like any website)          │
         │       ⬇                                                                │
         │  3️⃣  You click on "jobpost" in the Catalog                            │
         │       ⬇                                                                │
         │  4️⃣  Your browser asks the Backstage SERVER: "tell me about jobpost"  │
         │       ⬇                                                                │
         │  5️⃣  The server checks the database (PostgreSQL) for that entity      │
         │       ⬇                                                                │
         │  6️⃣  The server sends the answer back as data (JSON)                  │
         │       ⬇                                                                │
         │  7️⃣  Your browser turns that data into the page you see               │
         │                                                                        │
         │  🔑 THE ONE RULE TO REMEMBER: your browser NEVER talks to the          │
         │     database directly. It always asks the server first. Always.        │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    1.11 Backstage + Kubernetes — Two Different Relationships
         - This guide deploys Backstage ON Kubernetes from day one — but
           novices often confuse TWO separate relationships. Untangle them now.

         [THE DUAL RELATIONSHIP — Don't Mix These Up!]
         ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  RELATIONSHIP #1: Backstage RUNS ON Kubernetes                      │
         │  ───────────────────────────────────────────────                    │
         │  Backstage itself is just a Node.js app in a container.             │
         │  We deploy it as Pod(s) — this guide's Chapter 19 shows exactly     │
         │  how. K8s gives it self-healing, scaling, and rolling updates.      │
         │                                                                     │
         │  RELATIONSHIP #2: Backstage OBSERVES Kubernetes                     │
         │  ───────────────────────────────────────────────                    │
         │  The Kubernetes PLUGIN (Chapter 10, 20) lets Backstage DISPLAY      │
         │  live pod health for jobpost (or ANY service) on its entity page —  │
         │  regardless of where Backstage itself happens to run.               │
         │                                                                     │
         │  ⚠️ CBA GOTCHA: "Backstage runs on K8s" and "Backstage shows K8s    │
         │     data" are NOT the same fact — you can have either one           │
         │     without the other. The exam tests both separately.              │
         └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    1.12 Meet Your Demo Project — easypj & jobpost
         - Every concept in this guide is demonstrated on ONE real project.
         - Meet it now so nothing feels abstract later.

         [EASYPJ / JOBPOST — What You're Actually Looking At]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  🗂️  PROJECT:      easypj (a Django project)                      │
         │  📦 APPLICATION:  jobpost (a Django app inside it)                 │
         │  🎯 WHAT IT DOES:  Employers post jobs; users filter by            │
         │                    Skills, Location, and Author                    │
         │  🐍 STACK:         Django + PostgreSQL + Docker + Kubernetes       │
         │                                                                    │
         │  HOW IT WILL APPEAR IN BACKSTAGE (your roadmap for this guide):    │
         │  ┌────────────────────────┬───────────────────────────────────┐    │
         │  │  jobpost's...          │  ...becomes this in Backstage      │   │
         │  ├────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────┤    │
         │  │  Django app            │  Component entity      (Ch 3, 8)   │   │
         │  │  REST endpoints        │  API entity             (Ch 8, 9)  │   │
         │  │  PostgreSQL database   │  Resource entity        (Ch 3, 8)  │   │
         │  │  /docs folder          │  Published TechDocs     (Ch 14-15) │   │
         │  │  k8s/ manifests        │  Live pod health tab    (Ch 10, 20) │  │
         │  │  New feature branch    │  Scaffolded via Template (Ch 11-13) │  │
         │  └────────────────────────┴───────────────────────────────────┘    │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    1.13 Common Beginner Misconceptions — Myth vs Reality
         - New Backstage learners repeat the same handful of mix-ups.
           Clear them up before they become bad habits.

         [MYTH VS REALITY — Backstage Edition]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  ❌ MYTH                                ✅ REALITY                       │
         │  ─────────────────────────────────────  ──────────────────────────     │
         │  "Backstage is a monitoring tool"        It's a PORTAL that embeds     │
         │                                          monitoring tools via plugins  │
         │  "Backstage replaces Kubernetes"         It runs ON K8s and can also   │
         │                                          DISPLAY K8s data — not a K8s  │
         │                                          replacement                   │
         │  "You must rewrite your apps to use it"  You add ONE YAML file         │
         │                                          (catalog-info.yaml) — zero    │
         │                                          app code changes required     │
         │  "It's only for huge companies"          Any team with 10+ services    │
         │                                          already benefits from it      │
         │  "SQLite is fine for production"         SQLite = single-writer only.  │
         │                                          Production ALWAYS needs       │
         │                                          PostgreSQL (Ch 2, 4, 19)      │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

  [UNDER THE HOOD]
       The one-click walkthrough in 1.10 hides one crucial architectural
       fact worth restating on its own: your browser is NEVER trusted to
       talk to the database. Every single interaction -- clicking jobpost
       in the Catalog, running a Template, opening a TechDocs page -- is
       actually two hops: browser to server, server to database. It is
       this two-hop rule, not any particular feature, that lets Backstage
       add auth, caching, and multiple plugins in front of the same data
       without your browser code ever knowing the difference. Chapter 2
       names this properly as the frontend/backend/database split; here
       it is worth memorizing as a rule first: if you ever see a bug
       report where the UI reads or writes something impossible without a
       round trip to the server, that assumption is broken somewhere.

  [KEY TAKEAWAYS]
       - Backstage is an open source FRAMEWORK for building developer
         portals, created at Spotify, CNCF Incubating since 2022 -- NOT
         Graduated (1.2, 1.7).
       - The 4 Pillars -- Catalog, Templates, TechDocs, Plugins -- map to
         4 different questions: find, create, understand, extend (1.3,
         1.8).
       - Backstage's core vocabulary (Entity/Kind/Annotation/Plugin/
         Template) unlocks nearly every later chapter (1.9).
       - Your browser never talks to the database directly -- every
         action is browser -> server -> database (1.10).
       - "Runs on Kubernetes" and "displays Kubernetes data" are two
         separate relationships, not one (1.11).
       - easypj's jobpost Django app will become a Component + API +
         Resource in the Catalog (1.12).
       - A catalog-info.yaml is the ONLY app-code change required to
         onboard an existing service -- zero rewrites (1.13).

  [EXAM GOTCHAS]
       - Calling Backstage "a monitoring tool" or "a CNCF Graduated
         project" -- it's a PORTAL FRAMEWORK, and it is Incubating, not
         Graduated (1.2, 1.7).
       - Assuming "Backstage runs on K8s" implies "Backstage shows K8s
         data" (or vice versa) -- they're independent (1.11).
       - Confusing Labels with Annotations before Chapter 3 even
         introduces them formally -- 1.9 already plants the "sticky note"
         rule, don't skip it.
       - Thinking SQLite is fine for production because the create-app
         wizard defaults to it (1.13) -- it's single-writer, dev-only.

  [SPEED TIPS]
       - Memorize the one sentence in 1.9's info box -- it unlocks 80% of
         the vocabulary you'll need for Ch3.
       - When a CBA scenario question stumps you, ask "am I finding,
         creating, understanding, or extending?" -- that word IS the
         Pillar (1.8).

  [COMMANDS / CONFIG TO REMEMBER]
         ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │ BACKSTAGE — QUICK FACTS TO REMEMBER                                             │
         │                                                                                 │
         │ Definition: open source framework for building developer portals (backstage.io) │
         │ Origin: built at Spotify -> open-sourced 2020 -> donated to CNCF Sept 2020      │
         │ CNCF status: INCUBATING (since March 2022) -- NOT Graduated                     │
         │ Stack: TypeScript + React + Material UI (frontend); Node.js/Express (backend)   │
         │ The 4 Pillars: 📦 Catalog · 🚀 Templates · 📖 TechDocs · 🔌 Plugins             │
         │ Golden rule: Entity = a THING, Kind = its TYPE, Annotation = a sticky           │
         │              note that tells a Plugin where to find more info                   │
         └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

       - In 5 minutes, sketch the 4 Pillars diagram from memory and write

       - Write catalog-info.yaml's 4 top-level fields from memory (you'll
         meet the real syntax in Ch3).
       - Say out loud, without looking: is Backstage CNCF Graduated or
         Incubating? What year was it donated?
